Note.-Boats arriving from Tangyang pay full inward dues if discharging at Shasi town, local trade dues if discharging near the West Station; but in the Shipping table they are all entered as local traders. In the same way boats from Shasi town to Tangyang pay full outward dues, but are classed among local clearances. Boats arriving from Tangyang for other ports pay full outward dues. They are entered in the Shipping table first as local entries, then as interport clearances. Boats arriving from other ports for Tangyang have usually no dues to pay, but in the Shipping table they are entered, firstly, as interport entries and, secondly, as local clearances. PAR Ill. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended 81st December 1918.
292 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [OCT.-DEO., NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 559. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. NORTH CHANNEL ENTRANCE TO THE YANGTZE. WARNING REGARDING USE OF, BY DEEP-DRAUGHT VESSELS. NOTICE is hereby given that, owing to the liability of the Tsungming Crossing to rapid changes both as regards the course of the channel and the depth of water, deep-draught vessels are recommended not to use it without having previously ascertained from the Coast Inspector&#39;s Office that it is safe to do so. SHANGHAI, 22nd October 1913.
1913.] NOTIFICATIONS. 293 No. 560. YANGTZE RIVER-0HINKIANG AND SHANGHAI DISTRICTS. JOHNSON FLATS-LANGSHAN CROSSING. BUOY ESTABLISHED; UPPER CROSSING Buoy MOVED. NOTICE is hereby given that the following changes have been made in the buoyage of the eastern edge of the Johnson Flats:- A 6-foot conical buoy, painted black, and named the Pagoda Buoy, is now moored in 33 feet of water at low water of spring tides, to mark the eastern edge of the Johnson Flats. From the buoy, Langshan Pagoda bears N. 821* E., distant 3-9 miles. The Upper Crossing Buoy has been moved 1.5 cables to the eastward of its former position, owing to the extension of the Johnson Flats. The buoy is now moored in 28 feet of water at low water of spring tides. From the buoy, Langshan Pagoda bears N. 560 E., distant 5.25 miles. CAUTION. A recent examination of the Langshan Crossing shows that in the vicinity of the Upper Crossing Buoy the channel has now a breadth of only 31 cables between the 5-fathom contours, and that the Crossing in general is in a state of rapid change. All bearings given are magnetic. SHANGHAI, 28th October 1913.
294 CUSTOMS GAsETTE, [OCT.-DEC., No. 56 YANGTZE RIVER-SHA GHAI STRICT. APPROACHES TO THE WOOSUNG RIVER. QUARANTINE ACON SHIFTED. NOrTI is hereby given that, owing to the washing away of the western end of Chung- paosha, the Quarantine Beaon has been shifted. From its new position, Woosung Lighthouse bears S. 48&#39; W., magnetic, distant 3.55 miles. SHNAI, 27th November 1913. No. 562. SHANGHA DISTRICT. APPROACH TO THE SOUTH CHANNEL ENTRANCE TO THE YANGTZE. EXPERIMENTAL SUBMARINE BELL BUOY TO BE WITHDRAWN. NOTICE is hereby given that the experimental submarine bell buoy moored in the approach to the South Channel Entrance to the Yangtze will be withdrawn on or about the 22nd December 1913- SANouAI, i8th December 1913.
1913.] NOTIFlCATIONS. 29 No. 563. TIENTSI NDISTRICT. ENTRANCE TO THE HAIIO. ESrABSsMxNT Or RIVER MOUTH LEADING MARKS AND INWARD AND OUTWARn TURNING TRANarr MARKS. NOTICE is hereby given that the following aids to navigation have been established, to assist vessels in navigating between the Deep Hole and the entrance to the Haihe:- Two black pyramidal beacons to be known as the River Mouth Leading Marks, ha been established on the northern bank of the Iaiho, about I mile to to north-westward of the Fort Light. The high (or rear) beacon exhibits a white light. The low (or front) beacon exhibits a red light. The lights are fixed, dioptrie, of the sixth order. These beacons in transit mark the channel from the 8pit Light- boat &#39;toward the entance to the Haiho, until Oentral Fort (about 4 cables S.S.W. of Fort Light) is abeam. Three beacons, forming twe sets of unlighted transi marks anad to be known as the Inward &#39;ind Outward Turning Tranit Mars, have been established in the neighbourhood of the.,aku Bar Outer Leding Marks. The high (or rear) beacon is a black square. The low (or front) beacons are two black pyramids. The southernmost of these two sets of transit marks-the Inward Turning Transit Marks-indicate, when in line, where an inward-bound vessel should leave the Taku Bar Inner Leading Marks and proceed on the River Moth Leading Marks. The northernmost of these two sets of transit marks-the Outward Turning Transit Marks-indicate, when in line, where an outward- bound vessel should leave the River Mouth Leading Marks and proceed on the Taku Bar Inner Leading Marks. The customary withdrawal of the floating aids to navigation during the winter months is not affected by this Notice. The lighting of the several Leading Marks will be continued during the winter. SHANGHAI, 20th December 1913.
296 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [OCT.-DEC., 1913.] No. 564. YANGTZE RIVER-HANG,HAl DISTRICT. APPROACHES TO THE WOOSUNG RIVER: QUARANTINE ANCHORAGE. QUARANTINE LOWER BuOY MOVED AND ITS CHARACTERISTICS CHANGED. REFERRING to Special Notice to Mariners No. 408, notice is hereby given that the Quarantine Lower Buoy, Approaches to the Woosung River, has been moved, and its charao- teristics changed to a red buoy, gas-lighted, showing an occulting white light every 8 seconds, thus:- Light.......... .............4 seconds. Eclipse. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . 4 The buoy is now moored in 36 feet of water at low water of spring tides and marks the lower limit of the Quarantine Anchorage, the southern edge of the shoal to the northward of the Quarantine Anchorage, and the northern side of the obannel From the buoy, Woosung Lighthouse bears S. 74h W., magnetic, distant 2.64 miles.
